--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Mission Analysis:**
- Score increases by 1 per step for every robot OUTSIDE the target region (BLACK floor strip bordered by BLUE walls)
- Target: get all robots to the BLACK floor area near BLUE walls
- Robots need to: detect BLACK floor (both strips are black), distinguish BLUE vs GREEN walls, and aggregate in the correct strip

**Key perceptions:**
- Floor color: BLACK (both strips), GRAY (middle)
- Wall colors: BLUE (target strip), GREEN (other strip)
- C=7 can detect color 3 (blue?) - let me map colors: 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan
- So detecting blue walls (color 3) indicates the target region
- GoToColor with blue (clr=3) should move toward the target strip

**Strategy:**
1. Explore/wander to find the target area
2. When near blue walls (perceive blue, C=7 with l=3), move toward blue (GoToColor blue)
3. When on black floor (could be either strip), check for blue walls - if no blue visible, go away from green
4. Stay in blue strip once there

**Champion analysis:** Score 17917.44 with a spiral+repulsion+exploration FSM. This doesn't seem well-tuned for the actual task. Let me design task-specific machines.

**Better approach:**
- State 0: Exploration (find blue walls)
- State 1: GoToColor blue (move to target) 
- State 2: Stop (stay when on black floor near blue)

Or simpler: GoToColor blue always as main behavior, with exploration to escape local minima.

Let me design 14 machines:

1. **Champion (exact copy)**
2. **Exploitation variants of champion** (small edits)
3. **Task-focused designs** - GoToColor blue + exploration

For GoToColor blue (behavior 8, clr=3): moves toward blue walls = target region. This seems like the core strategy.

GoAwayColor green (behavior 9, clr=2): moves away from green walls = also toward target.

## Reasoning

**Champion Analysis:**
The champion (score 5222.85) uses 4 states:
- S0: Exploration (rwm=35) - explores, transitions to S1 if sees blue (color 3), to S2 if sees green (color 2), or to S2 if on black floor
- S1: GoToColor blue (target!) - transitions to S2 if on black floor, or back to S0 if on gray
- S2: GoAwayColor green - transitions to S1 if sees blue, or to S0 if on gray
- S3: Stop - transitions back based on floor/color perception

This is a good strategy: find blue walls → go to them; avoid green walls; stop when in target.

**Key insight:** The champion already has a solid structure. The core logic:
1. Explore until you perceive blue or green walls
2. Go toward blue (target) or away from green (which also leads toward blue)
3. Stop when on black floor near blue walls

**Exploitation:** Tweak probabilities and parameters of the champion.
**Exploration:** Try different topologies like 2-3 state machines with similar logic, or adding attraction to help robots cluster.

**LED color fix:** Only 0, 4, 5, 6 are valid (not 3).

Let me design the 14 machines:

1. Champion (exact copy)
2-7. Exploitation variants (small tweaks to probabilities/gains)
8-14. Exploration variants (different topologies)

## Reasoning

**Champion Analysis (score 2977.56):**
- S0: GoAwayColor green (vel=1.0, clr=2) → if sees blue go to S1, if on black go to S1, if on gray stay (prob 0.05 loop back)
- S1: GoToColor blue (vel=1.0, clr=3) → if on black STAY in S1 (self-loop via transition 0), if on gray go to S0
- S2: Stop → if on gray go to S0, if sees blue go to S1, if sees green go to S0

Wait, S1 transition 0: destination index 1 means... with 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0,2]. So D=0→state0, D=1→state2. So:
- S1 transition 0: D=1 → state 2 (Stop) when on black floor! 
- S1 transition 1: D=0 → state 0 when on gray floor

So the flow is: GoAwayGreen → (see blue or on black) → GoToBlue → (on black) → Stop
Stop re-engages exploration if on gray or sees colors.

This makes sense! Robots move away from green, find blue walls, go to them, then stop on black floor near blue.

**Key insight:** S1 self-references via destination index. With 3 states, leaving state 1: others=[0,2], so D=0→S0, D=1→S2.

**Exploitation strategy:**
- Tweak probabilities (0.9→0.95, 0.8→0.85, etc.)
- Try different rwm/vel values
- Add extra transitions to states

**Exploration strategy:**
- 4-state versions adding an exploration state
- Different orderings
- Add neighbor-based conditions

Valid LED colors: 0, 4, 5, 6 only.

Let me be very careful with destination indices:
- 3 states: leaving S0, others=[1,2]: D=0→S1, D=1→S2
- 3 states: leaving S1, others=[0,2]: D=0→S0, D=1→S2
- 3 states: leaving S2, others=[0,1]: D=0→S0, D=1→S1
- 4 states: leaving S0, others=[1,2,3]: D=0→S1, D=1→S2, D=2→S3
- 4 states: leaving S1, others=[0,2,3]: D=0→S0, D=1→S2, D=2→S3
- 4 states: leaving S2, others=[0,1,3]: D=0→S0, D=1→S1, D=2→S3
- 4 states: leaving S3, others=[0,1,2]: D=0→S0, D=1→S1, D=2→S2

**Champion Analysis (score 2602.93):**
- S0: GoAwayColor green → if sees blue→S1, if on black→S1, if on gray (prob 0.03) stay
- S1: GoToColor blue → if on black→S2 (stop, prob 0.98), if on gray→S0 (prob 0.05)
- S2: Stop → if on gray→S0 (prob 0.3), if sees blue→S1 (prob 0.8), if sees green→S0 (prob 0.6)

The logic: avoid green walls, seek blue walls, stop when on black floor near blue. If displaced to gray, re-engage.

**Key observations:**
- Very high probability of stopping on black (0.98) is good
- Low gray-escape from S0 (0.03) keeps robots away from green strip
- S2 stop state re-engages well

**Exploitation:** Fine-tune probabilities around champion values.
**Exploration:** Try 4-state versions, different behavior combinations.

**LED color reminder:** Only 0, 4, 5, 6 are valid.
**Destination index reminder:** With N states, leaving state I, destinations are indexed among the OTHER states only.

Let me carefully verify destination indices for all machines:
- 3 states, leaving S0: others=[1,2], D=0→S1, D=1→S2

- 4 states, leaving S2: others=[0,1,3], D=0→S0, D=1→S1, D=2→S3

## Reasoning

**Champion Analysis (score 2585.39):**
- S0: GoAwayColor green → sees blue→S1 (p=0.88), on black→S1 (p=0.82), on gray→S0 (p=0.03, stay)
- S1: GoToColor blue → on black→S2 (p=0.98), on gray→S0 (p=0.05)
- S2: Stop → on gray→S0 (p=0.28), sees blue→S1 (p=0.82), sees green→S0 (p=0.58)

This is a very clean and effective design. The score improved only marginally from 2602 to 2585, suggesting we're near a local optimum for this topology.

**Strategy:**
- Exploitation: Fine-tune probabilities around champion values (±5-10%)
- Exploration: Try 4-state extensions, different S2 behaviors (attraction instead of stop), modified topologies

- LED colors: only 0, 4, 5, 6
- Destination indices are among OTHER states only

- 4 states: S0→[1,2,3]; S1→[0,2,3]; S2→[0,1,3]; S3→[0,1,2]

## Reasoning

**Situation:** Champion unchanged at 2585.39. We're stuck in a local optimum. Need more aggressive exploration while keeping the champion alive.

**Champion structure:**
- S0: GoAwayGreen → S1 if sees blue (p=0.88) or on black (p=0.82), back to S0 if on gray (p=0.03)
- S1: GoToBlue → S2 if on black (p=0.98), back to S0 if on gray (p=0.05)
- S2: Stop → S0 if on gray (p=0.28), S1 if sees blue (p=0.82), S0 if sees green (p=0.58)

**Issues with current approach:**
- We've been tweaking probabilities without improvement
- Need genuinely different topologies
- The "stop" behavior in S2 means robots that reached the target stay there, which is good
- But maybe FlockCenter or Attraction in the target area would help gather more robots

**New ideas to explore:**
1. Replace S2 Stop with FlockCenter to pull other robots together
2. Add a 4th state for neighbor-based clustering in the target zone
3. Try different exploration behaviors (ZigZag, Spiral) in S0
4. Try GoToBlue directly as main behavior with GoAwayGreen as secondary
5. Use neighbor count conditions to detect when in the strip (many neighbors = stay)

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Situation:** Champion unchanged at 2585.39 for 3 rounds. We're deeply stuck. Need radical exploration while preserving the champion.

**Analysis of why we're stuck:**
- The 3-state GoAwayGreen→GoToBlue→Stop topology seems to be a local optimum
- Small tweaks aren't helping
- Need genuinely different approaches

**New ideas:**
1. The score penalizes robots OUTSIDE the target (black floor near blue walls). Both strips have black floors, so robots might stop in the WRONG black strip. The key differentiator is blue vs green walls.
2. What if robots use color detection more aggressively? Maybe a robot on black floor that still sees green should flee.
3. What about using the neighbor count to create a "beacon" effect - robots that found the target stay and attract others?
4. The stop state (S2) might be wrong if robots are in the wrong black strip. Need to escape green-bordered strip.

**Key insight I may have missed:** Both black strips look the same to floor sensors. Only wall colors differ. A robot stopped in the GREEN strip is penalized. The champion's S2 transitions: "sees green → go to S0 (GoAwayGreen)" helps escape the wrong strip!

**New topology ideas:**
- A robot on black that sees green should immediately flee - make this very high probability
- Try FlockCenter as the "aggregation" behavior in the target zone
- Try a 4-state with explicit "wrong strip escape" state
- Try using only GoToBlue (no stop) - keep moving toward blue walls

**Destination index reminder (critical):**
- 3 states, S0 leaves to [S1,S2]: D=0→S1, D=1→S2
- 3 states, S1 leaves to [S0,S2]: D=0→S0, D=1→S2  
- 3 states, S2 leaves to [S0,S1]: D=0→S0, D=1→S1
- 4 states, S0→[S1,S2,S3]: D=0,1,2; S1→[S0,S2,S3]: D=0,1,2; S2→[S0,S1,S3]: D=0,1,2; S3→[S0,S1,S2]: D=0,1,2

**LED: only 0, 4, 5, 6**

FSM: --nstates 3 --s0 9 --vel0 1.0 --cle0 5 --clr0 2 --n0 3 --n0x0 1 --c0x0 7 --p0x0 0.88 --l0x0 3 --n0x1 1 --c0x1 0 --p0x1 0.82 --n0x2 0 --c0x2 1 --p0x2 0.03 --s1 8 --vel1 1.0 --cle1 0 --clr1 3 --n1 2 --n1x0 1 --c1x0 0 --p1x0 0.98 --n1x1 0 --c1x1 1 --p1x1 0.05 --s2 1 --cle2 0 --n2 3 --n2x0 0 --c2x0 1 --p2x0 0.28 --n2x1 1 --c2x1 7 --p2x1 0.82 --l2x1 3 --n2x2 0 --c2x2 7 --p2x2 0.58 --l2x2 2

## Reasoning

**Situation:** Champion stuck at 2585.39 for 5 rounds. The same errors keep appearing in my submissions (cle=3 invalid, missing n3x2 destination). Let me be extremely careful this round.

**Root cause of errors I keep making:**
- I keep using `--cle=3` (blue LED) which is INVALID. Valid: 0, 4, 5, 6 only.
- For 4-state machines, S3 has transitions n3x0, n3x1, n3x2 - I must include `--n3x2 D` with a destination.

**Champion logic recap:**
- S0: GoAwayGreen → S1 if sees blue(p=0.88) or on black(p=0.82), back to S0 if gray(p=0.03)
- S1: GoToBlue → S2(stop) if on black(p=0.98), back to S0 if gray(p=0.05)  
- S2: Stop → S0 if gray(p=0.28), S1 if sees blue(p=0.82), S0 if sees green(p=0.58)

**Why stuck?** The FSM is near optimal for this topology. Need genuinely different ideas.

**New approaches to try:**
1. The "stop" in S2 is good but maybe FlockCenter would pull more robots in
2. What if S2 used GoToColor blue with very low prob to drift toward walls?
3. Try making S1 (GoToBlue) transition to S0 (GoAwayGreen) when it sees green - avoids wrong strip
4. 4-state: add a "waiting/beacon" state that uses attraction to pull others
5. Completely different: use only GoToBlue as primary behavior, never stop

**Careful index mapping for 4 states:**
- S3 leaving: others=[S0,S1,S2], so D=0→S0, D=1→S1, D=2→S2
- Must include ALL declared transitions with destination + condition + params

Let me write 13 valid machines:

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
